# my-CK2-come

Private snapshot of a Crusader Kings II install (**3.3.5.1**), kept in Git for personal
use — plus an analysis of the CleanSlate mod built against it.

- `Crusader Kings II/` — the game folder. ~15,700 files, ~420 MB tracked.
- History is one squashed commit by design. GitHub web uploads add
  "Add files via upload" commits — squash again whenever that gets noisy.

## Latest comparison work

[**Family references and exceptional claim provenance**](CleanSlate-Archive/06-session-records/13-family-and-exceptional-claim-provenance.md)
— 18 selected cases: 10 parent-verified transitions and eight earliest-observed
states. This addendum traces the missing fathers/Mani mismatch, Tangier, Hamadan,
three claim removals and Kabulistan's internal upstream retarget. **139 tests pass;
six offline outputs reproduce byte-for-byte.** Sources remain untouched;
historical/runtime questions are still explicit.
[Earlier employer/date register](CleanSlate-Archive/06-session-records/12-employer-date-provenance-and-register.md) ·
[Current queue](CleanSlate-Archive/06-session-records/04-open-work.md).

### The analysis in one line

CleanSlate is a rewrite of vanilla CK2's script layer (661+ bug fixes, no balance
changes). The code is in excellent health — 0 duplicate event IDs where vanilla has 1,388,
0 duplicate localisation keys where vanilla has 265, 0 dangling **event** references, 0 orphaned
history files in the earlier scoped checks. These are not a clean bill of health
for character genealogy: the current family pass finds two unresolved CS father
IDs and additional chronology/scope concerns. Its **documentation** is the weak point: the upstream GitHub front page has
been frozen since 2021-04-13 while 983 commits of fixes landed behind it.
→ [Executive summary](CleanSlate-Archive/00-overview/executive-summary.md)

### Version, for the record

The install is **3.3.5.1**. `ChangeLog.txt` stops at 3.3.3 because Paradox never wrote
entries for the 3.3.4/3.3.5 hotfixes — don't trust it. The real version string lives in
`CK2game.exe` next to the Jenkins build path.

## Looks broken, is intentional

- **9,568 zero-byte files** — all art/audio outside `map/` (`.dds`, `.tga`,
  `.wav`, `.mp3`, `.ogg`, `.xac`, `.xsm`, `.mesh`, movies). Deliberate
  placeholders: a full CK2+DLC install is ~4 GB, far over GitHub's limits.
  `map/`, `common/`, `events/`, `history/`, `interface/`, `localisation/`,
  `tutorial/`, `pdx_browser/` and the EXE/DLLs are real.
- **`dlc/dlcNNN.zip` (73 files)** — CK2's native DLC containers, referenced by
  the `.dlc` descriptors (`archive = "dlc/dlcNNN.zip"`). Never unpack them.
- **`pdx_browser/libcef.dll`** — 87,382,528 B, CEF 3.2840.1493.0 (x64),
  statically imported by `PDXBrowser.dll`. 83 MiB is under GitHub's 100 MiB
  hard limit; the GH001 push warning is expected.
  SHA-256 `cc546e764ff47a8d3f251e13f6bc6f245baa9b725f14e0f896a4bc98e6a75ea9`.

### Recovering what the stripping removed

`ck2-hashes.csv` was generated from a **full, unstripped** install, so the 9,535 files
that are 0 bytes here are still identified by hash. That answers "was this texture changed
or merely renamed?" without keeping 3.9 GB. Verify the current folder against it with:

```bash
python3 ck2-hash.py --verify ck2-hashes.csv
```

Against this stripped repo that run reports, and all of it is expected:

| Line | Count | Why |
|---|---:|---|
| `changed content` | **70** | all `dlc/*.zip` — repacked without inner art (973.8 MB full vs 4.2 MB here) |
| `emptied (now 0 bytes)` | **9,535** | the art/audio placeholders, by design |
| `missing since manifest` | **83** | ebooks, EULAs, license notices never committed here |
| `added since manifest` | **0** | nothing here is unaccounted for |

Every other file — 6,049 of them, the whole script layer — is bit-identical to the full
install.

## Adding a big file

1. Pack it as split RAR/7z volumes (≤ 25 MB each for web upload) and upload
   them to the repo root.
2. Extract, place, verify (size / hash / PE headers), delete the volumes,
   then squash history so the archive blobs don't stay buried in it.

## Notes for agents

- Arena clones are **shallow**. Run `git fetch --unshallow origin` before
  judging history — one session declared history clean after seeing only the
  tip commit, while 27 MiB of dead RAR blobs sat underneath.
- Session transcripts are **not kept** at the root. Each new one gets
  shredded into the archive's knowledge base and then deleted; the routing
  map and Git recovery point are in
  `CleanSlate-Archive/06-session-records/14-transcript-provenance.md`.
- The sandbox can be re-cloned between turns and **unpushed commits do not
  survive** — commit early, push immediately, and re-verify against committed
  artifacts after any reset.
- The sandbox has no `unrar`/`7z`; apt and GitHub's release-asset CDN are
  blocked, but PyPI and `codeload.github.com` work. Build 7-Zip from source:
  `curl -L https://codeload.github.com/ip7z/7zip/tar.gz/refs/tags/26.03 | tar xz`,
  then `make -f ../../cmpl_gcc.mak` inside `CPP/7zip/Bundles/Alone2` →
  `b/g/7zz` (handles RAR5 multi-volume). Store-mode RAR4 volumes can be read
  with pure-Python `rarfile` without any binary.
- Rewriting `main` needs a force-push, which a session pinned to its own branch
  cannot do. Prepare the result on the session branch, then swap the default
  branch in GitHub → Settings → Branches.
=== COPYING-OTHER-REPOS ===
# Copying someone else's repo into yours (without downloading anything)

Short answer: **yes**, and you never touch a zip. GitHub copies repo→repo on its own
servers. Three ways, depending on what you want.

## Which one do you want?

| | Fork | Mirror (`gh-mirror.sh`) | Pull into *this* repo |
|---|---|---|---|
| Effort | one click | one command | one command |
| Result | linked copy | standalone repo you own | extra branch **here** |
| Keeps full history | yes | yes | yes |
| Shows "forked from …" | **yes** | no | no |
| Can do the same repo twice | no | yes | yes |
| Counts against your storage | no | yes | yes |
| **Can the agent do it for you?** | no¹ | no¹ | **yes** |

¹ My sandbox token is a bot (`arena-ai-coding-agent[bot]`). It can **read** any public
repo and **write to this one**, but it cannot create repos in your account:

```
$ gh repo create UselessMoron123/_scratch_test --private
GraphQL: Resource not accessible by integration (createRepository)
```

So options 1 and 2 are a single command **you** run; option 3 I can do right now.

---

## Option 1 — Fork (simplest)

On <https://github.com/ck2plus/CK2Plus> press **Fork**. Or:

```bash
gh repo fork ck2plus/CK2Plus --clone=false
```

Instant, uses none of your storage, and you can pull upstream updates later. Downside:
it's permanently labelled *"forked from ck2plus/CK2Plus"*, and GitHub allows only one fork
of a given repo per account.

## Option 2 — Mirror into a repo you own

Use `gh-mirror.sh` in this repo. It creates the new repo and pushes a complete copy —
every branch, tag, commit and author — server-to-server.

```bash
./gh-mirror.sh ck2plus/CK2Plus                  # -> UselessMoron123/CK2Plus (private)
./gh-mirror.sh ck2plus/CK2Plus my-ck2plus       # choose the name
./gh-mirror.sh --public ck2plus/CK2Plus         # make it public
./gh-mirror.sh --all ck2plus                    # every repo in the org
```

It refuses to overwrite an existing repo, and strips `refs/pull/*` before pushing (GitHub
rejects those, and a plain `git push --mirror` would fail without this).

**A mirror is a snapshot.** To refresh it later:

```bash
git clone --mirror https://github.com/ck2plus/CK2Plus.git tmp.git
git -C tmp.git push --mirror https://github.com/UselessMoron123/CK2Plus.git
rm -rf tmp.git
```

## Option 3 — Bring the history into *this* repo

Keeps everything in one place. Verified working — I fetched **1,589 commits** of
`ck2plus/CK2Plus` into this clone in ~17 s.

**As a branch** (history stays separate, nothing mixes with your files):

```bash
git remote add ck2plus https://github.com/ck2plus/CK2Plus.git
git fetch ck2plus main
git branch ck2plus-main ck2plus/main
git push origin ck2plus-main
```

**As a subfolder** (`CK2Plus/` alongside `Crusader Kings II/`):

```bash
git subtree add --prefix=CK2Plus https://github.com/ck2plus/CK2Plus.git main --squash
```

`--squash` collapses their history into one commit — keeps your repo small. Drop it to
keep all 1,589 commits interleaved in your log.

## Licensing note

These are public repos, so copying is technically fine, but none of the CKPlus
repositories ship a `LICENSE` file. "Public" is not the same as "freely reusable" — mirror
for study and archival, and ask the authors before redistributing anything derived from
them.
=== gh-mirror.sh ===
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# gh-mirror.sh - copy someone else's GitHub repo into your own account,
#                server-to-server. Nothing is downloaded to your machine.
#
# Needs: gh (already authenticated) and git.
#
# USAGE
#   ./gh-mirror.sh ck2plus/CK2Plus                     # -> <you>/CK2Plus (private)
#   ./gh-mirror.sh ck2plus/CK2Plus my-ck2plus-copy     # choose the new name
#   ./gh-mirror.sh --public ck2plus/CK2Plus            # make it public
#   ./gh-mirror.sh --all ck2plus                       # mirror EVERY repo in that org
#
# WHAT IT DOES
#   Creates a new repo under your account and pushes a full mirror into it:
#   every branch, every tag, the complete commit history and authorship.
#   Uses --mirror, so it is a true copy, not a shallow snapshot.
#
# WHY NOT "FORK"?
#   A fork is one click and uses no storage, but it stays visibly attached to
#   the upstream repo and you cannot fork the same repo twice into one account.
#   A mirror is a standalone repo you fully own. Pick whichever you prefer:
#   for a fork just run:  gh repo fork OWNER/NAME --clone=false

set -euo pipefail

VIS="--private"
ALL=0
ARGS=()

while [ $# -gt 0 ]; do
  case "$1" in
    --public)  VIS="--public"; shift ;;
    --private) VIS="--private"; shift ;;
    --all)     ALL=1; shift ;;
    -h|--help) sed -n '2,28p' "$0"; exit 0 ;;
    *)         ARGS+=("$1"); shift ;;
  esac
done

command -v gh  >/dev/null || { echo "error: gh not found"       >&2; exit 1; }
command -v git >/dev/null || { echo "error: git not found"      >&2; exit 1; }
gh auth status >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo "error: run 'gh auth login' first" >&2; exit 1; }

ME=$(gh api user --jq .login)
TMP=$(mktemp -d)
trap 'rm -rf "$TMP"' EXIT

mirror_one() {
  local SRC="$1" DEST="${2:-}"
  local SRC_NAME="${SRC##*/}"
  DEST="${DEST:-$SRC_NAME}"

  echo "=============================================================="
  echo "  $SRC  ->  $ME/$DEST"
  echo "=============================================================="

  if ! gh api "repos/$SRC" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    echo "  ! source repo not found or not visible: $SRC" >&2
    return 1
  fi

  local SIZE DESC
  SIZE=$(gh api "repos/$SRC" --jq '.size')          # KB
  DESC=$(gh api "repos/$SRC" --jq '.description // ""')
  echo "  source size: $(( SIZE / 1024 )) MB"

  if gh api "repos/$ME/$DEST" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    echo "  ! $ME/$DEST already exists - skipping (delete it or pick another name)" >&2
    return 1
  fi

  echo "  creating $ME/$DEST ..."
  gh repo create "$ME/$DEST" $VIS \
     --description "Mirror of $SRC. $DESC" >/dev/null

  echo "  cloning (bare mirror) ..."
  git clone --quiet --mirror "https://github.com/$SRC.git" "$TMP/$DEST.git"

  # A mirror clone also carries refs GitHub refuses on push (pull request refs).
  # Strip them so the push does not fail.
  git -C "$TMP/$DEST.git" for-each-ref --format='%(refname)' 'refs/pull/*' \
    | while read -r ref; do git -C "$TMP/$DEST.git" update-ref -d "$ref"; done

  echo "  pushing full history ..."
  git -C "$TMP/$DEST.git" push --quiet --mirror "https://github.com/$ME/$DEST.git"

  local B T C
  B=$(git -C "$TMP/$DEST.git" for-each-ref --format='%(refname)' refs/heads | wc -l)
  T=$(git -C "$TMP/$DEST.git" for-each-ref --format='%(refname)' refs/tags  | wc -l)
  C=$(git -C "$TMP/$DEST.git" rev-list --count --all)
  rm -rf "$TMP/$DEST.git"

  echo "  done: $C commits, $B branches, $T tags"
  echo "  https://github.com/$ME/$DEST"
  echo
}

if [ "$ALL" -eq 1 ]; then
  ORG="${ARGS[0]:?usage: $0 --all ORG}"
  echo "Mirroring every repo in '$ORG' to '$ME' ..."
  echo
  gh repo list "$ORG" --limit 200 --json nameWithOwner --jq '.[].nameWithOwner' \
  | while read -r repo; do
      mirror_one "$repo" || true
    done
else
  SRC="${ARGS[0]:?usage: $0 OWNER/NAME [NEW_NAME]}"
  mirror_one "$SRC" "${ARGS[1]:-}"
fi

echo "All done."

# CleanSlate — Analysis Archive

An organised, cross-referenced study of the **CleanSlate** mod for Crusader Kings II:
what the authors were doing, how the codebase is built, how healthy it is, and where
it could be improved.

## Latest character-history comparison

[**Family and exceptional claim provenance (3g, third pass)**](06-session-records/13-family-and-exceptional-claim-provenance.md)
adds 18 selected cases to the working register without rewriting earlier evidence:
10 verified transitions, eight earliest-state observations, and coupled title
context. No guessed genealogy or runtime verdicts.
[Earlier employer/date register](06-session-records/12-employer-date-provenance-and-register.md) ·
[Stage index](06-session-records/INDEX.md) · [Remaining work](06-session-records/04-open-work.md).

Built by comparing three sources against each other:

| Source | What it is |
|---|---|
| `CleanSlate-main.zip` (repo root) | the mod as uploaded here — 6,419 files |
| `ck2plus/CleanSlate` upstream git | 2,172 commits, 2019-12-18 → 2026-06-29, 18 contributors |
| `Crusader Kings II/` (repo root) | a real vanilla **3.3.5.1** install, used as the diff baseline |
| `characters/` (repo root) | the repaired working copy — vanilla + the 17 ID fixes from [`ID_FIXES.md`](../ID_FIXES.md) — the third comparison tree |

> **Verified:** the uploaded zip is **byte-identical** to upstream `HEAD` = `7ee3cbae`

> locally diverged — `diff -rq` reports **0** differences across all 6,419 files.

## The 60-second version

CleanSlate is **not** a gameplay mod. It is a *rewrite of vanilla CK2's entire script layer*
— every event, decision, trigger and effect re-typed to one house style, with ~661+
documented bug fixes applied and **zero intentional balance changes**. It exists to be the
clean foundation other mods (notably CK2+) build on.

**The code is in excellent health.** Measured, not assumed:

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Brace balance, 5,679 script files | **1** unbalanced — and it's a `.info` doc, not game script |
| Duplicate event IDs | **0** (vanilla itself ships 1,388 collisions) |
| Dangling event references | **0** of 10,144 |
| Duplicate localisation keys | **0** (vanilla ships 265) |
| Localisation regressions | **0** — every one of 3,130 dropped vanilla keys is genuinely unused |
| Orphaned title-history files | **0** of 2,757 |

**The documentation is not.** The repo's front page — `README.md`, which *calls itself*
"Master Changelog" — was last updated **2021-04-13**. Since then **983 commits** have
changed game script, and a second, live changelog (`CleanSlate/master_changelog.txt`,
105 KB) has quietly become the real one. A visitor to the GitHub page is reading a
document that is five years and ~922 lines out of date, including **323 renamed title
IDs** — exactly the save-breaking information downstream modders most need.

Full detail, with the fix, in [§5 Changelog audit](04-changelog/changelog-audit.md).

## Scope note

This archive **analyses**; it does not patch the mod. The mod files under
`CleanSlate-main.zip` are untouched. Everything here is new, additive documentation.

################ ea6f4af7
DATE: 2026-09-26T15:54:46Z
AUTHOR: Whizzer <809596+Whizzer@users.noreply.github.com>
MSG:
More republic CB sanity clean-up

The republic CB to take over a non-capital city holding in a rival republic can now be used if the city is not directly held by a patrician, but only if the city is not located in a county that's part of the defender, that is only outposts, as that is the spirit of this CB and the seize trade post CB, see below
The republic CB to seize a trade post can no longer be used on trade posts that are inside a rival republic, since hardcoding would destroy the trade post - it's not possible to hold a trade post inside a rival republic's province.

=== ADDED LINES (first 400) ===
+Number of noteworthy bugs and oversights fixed: 908
+All event-driven deaths occur instantly, rather than on clicking the event option
+Patched up trade post inaccuracies due to map changes, closely matching that of The Republic's original release
+Restored the Contarini family in the Republic of Venice
+Reminded historical republics that a trade post can be built in Amalfi
+Extended Viking guidance to cover the southern half of France, as per history
+Creating the HRE and restoring the Roman Empire now requires being feudal, since their special succession laws are clearly feudal only
+Creating custom kingdoms and empires now makes your current capital the de jure capital of the newly created title
+Allowed imperial vassals to change the succession laws on the kingdom title they created themselves, which only seems fair.
+Removed DLC checks to ways to free tributaries, so that tributaries in title history can be removed through normal gameplay
+The pope can now absolve tribal kinslaying too
+
+Properly opened up merchant republics to women, rather than only through the loopholes and openings Paradox presumably was unaware of. Due to hardcoding issues, agnatic-cognatic and enatic-cognatic are not available. The minor title to override the seniority heir is now properly restricted to the family palace gender succession law.
+- Merchant republic AI may now choose Equality and Enatic Clans when reforming a religion (they could already choose Harmonious).
+- Decisions to become a merchant republic, whether as tribal, nomadic, or to become the Hansa, can now be picked by women, and can pick female vassals as patricians. Upon doing so, agnatic-cognatic and enatic-cognatic are changed to absolute cognatic if eligible to do so manually.
+- Changing the gender succession law in the primary title of a patrician will now also set that law on the patrician's family palace, to ensure smooth successions.
+- Added female cultural titles for female leaders of merchant republics
+- Added female equivalent to 'Patriciate' government flavour
+- Alternate start female patricians can now also start with cognatic
+
+Unhardcoded various reformed pagan religion features:
+ - Invasions and cheap raiding are distinctive features of Norse paganism, yet are part of its special religion feature, instead of being inherently part of the reformed religion. This increases the amount of freedom and meaningful choices that can be made in reforming a religion. The same should be possible with all other pagan reformed religions, hence the following:
+   - Ritual Sacrifice (Bloodthirsty Gods) and Pyramids are no longer inherently reformed Aztec, have been added to the special Aztec feature, and are also available separately to Aztec pagan reformers
+   - Animistic is no longer inherently reformed West African, has been added to the special West African feature, and is also available separately to West African pagan reformers
+   - Haruspicy and Astrology are no longer inherently reformed Hellenic, have been added to the special Hellenic feature, and are also available separately to Hellenic pagan reformers
+ - Additional checks have been added to make these religions' special features incompatible with other features to due redundancy and incompatibility.
+ - Additional ai_will_do modifiers have been added to these individual features, analogous to adventuring and seafaring's modifiers for Norse pagans.
+The Mongol Empire's landless status is now unset upon loading save game if the Mongol emperor is, in fact, landed
+Fixed number of failed Northern Crusades resetting upon the Teutonic Order's Grandmaster's death - it's now tracked globally
+Added missing indian_group interface religion screen backgrounds for use with different religion features
+Ensured no one is ever the tributary of more than one tributary, in various CBs
+The features tooltip for nomadic government now mentions the possiblity to reform to tribal government
+Tribal Reformed Bön pagans no longer dislike tribal organisation, like most of their other reformed pagan brethren
+Fixed potentially incorrect tooltips in add_scarred_tiered_effect
+The 'Shut the gates'-decision no longer shows up when already secluded
+The spouse in 'Spouse suggests new councillor'-events can no longer suggest themselves
+AI will no longer send away smiths that were sought to create crown jewels (MNM.9100)
+Fixed feasts not clearing all flags
+The landless-property of pagan religion head titles is now set on reformation, if religion head is theocratic or autocephalous, and maintained by event on loading a saved game. Allows these titles to be destroyed and re-created if the religion is reformed with a secular head of the religion.
+Copied ai_will_do from order_to_take_vows to order_to_join_holy_order, so that AI won't use it randomly
+Corrected "de Neufmarché" dynasty name
+Fixed a few issues in the child baptism decision and associated events

+The decadence usurpation CB now requires the attacker to not be decadent, and invalidates of the attacker becomes decadent, or if the defender is no longer decadent
+Fixed some leaders of merchant republics being unplayable, due to missing family palace titles, a missing dynasty and many mistakes in title history
+Made unheld family palace titles unactive, so that they don't show up in the in-game title finder
+Opened up the coastal_republic CB up to other merchant republics in the same realm
+The coastal_county_republic CB now requires the owned holding to be a city, as mentioned in its description
+coastal_republic can now invalidate if the war requirements are no longer satisfied, and now also protects against taking cities in the capital of a merchant republic if said merchant republic is a subject of the primary war defender.
+coastal_county_republic can now be used against fellow vassals, just like coastal_republic, which only seems sensible.
+embargo_cb now only allows to embargo against CBs owned by merchant republics, and will now invalidate if somehow the involved trade posts provinces leave the realm.
+seize_trade_post's restriction against weaker patricians now only works against weaker patricians in the same merchant republic. Other fellow vassal merchant republics are fair game.
+The republic CB to take over a non-capital city holding in a rival republic can now be used if the city is not directly held by a patrician, but only if the city is not located in a county that's part of the defender, that is only outposts, as that is the spirit of this CB and the seize trade post CB, see below
+The republic CB to seize a trade post can no longer be used on trade posts that are inside a rival republic, since hardcoding would destroy the trade post - it's not possible to hold a trade post inside a rival republic's province.

# The changelog situation

← [Archive index](../README.md)

**This is the most significant problem in the project, and it is not a code problem.**

You asked whether the existing changelog captures everything. It does not — but the
interesting part is *why*: there are **two** changelogs, they have silently diverged, and
the stale one is the one the public sees.

## The two files

| | `README.md` | `CleanSlate/master_changelog.txt` |
|---|---|---|
| Where | repo root — **GitHub's front page** | buried inside the mod folder |
| Titled | *"CleanSlate / Master Changelog"* | *"CleanSlate beta 6.1"* |
| Size | 63,577 B · 1,032 lines | **105,248 B · 2,044 lines** |
| Last content update | **2021-04-13** (`43b93a64`) | **2026-06-29** (`7ee3cbae`) |
| Commits touching it since `1.0-beta6.1` | **0** | 15 |
| Status | frozen at beta 1 | current with `HEAD` |

The README was created by commit *"Update ReadMe to be the Master Changelog"* on
2021-04-13 — the same day `1.0-beta` was tagged — and has not been substantively touched
since. In the **five years** after that, **983 commits changed game script**.

`master_changelog.txt` is genuinely current: I verified **0 script commits** land after
its last update. The authors have kept a changelog faithfully. It's just not the one on
the front page.

## What a README reader never sees

**922 lines** of changelog content exist only in `master_changelog.txt`.

### Entire DLC sections are missing

`master_changelog.txt` has 17 top-level sections. The README has 5.

| In both | **Missing from README** |
|---|---|
| For players | Sword of Islam · Legacy of Rome · Sunset Invasion |
| Jade Dragon | The Republic · The Old Gods · Sons of Abraham |
| Holy Fury | Rajas of India · Charlemagne · Way of Life |
| LT · For modders | Horse Lords · Conclave · The Reaper's Due |

**11 of 16 DLC sections are absent** from the public document.

### The renamed IDs — the part that actually breaks things

| | master_changelog | README |
|---|---:|---:|
| Rename pairs documented | **425** | 40 |
| …of which are title IDs | **327** | **2** |

**325 title renames are undocumented on the front page.** These are the changes that break
saves and break dependent mods:

```
e_russia   -> e_rus          k_dacia     -> k_wallachia
e_germany  -> e_germania     d_slesvig   -> d_jylland
e_france   -> e_francia      d_armenia_minor -> d_cilicia
e_turkestan-> e_turan        c_domazlice -> c_south_bohemia
```

Plus **86 deleted obsolete titles** (`k_al-murabitids`, …), also README-absent.

### Volume by category

| Content only in `master_changelog.txt` | Lines |
|---|---:|
| Bugfixes | 116 |
| Deleted unused/obsolete titles | 86 |
| Scripted trigger/effect changes | 72 |
| Trait changes | 39 |
| The Reaper's Due section | 30 |
| Localisation changes | 24 |
| Holy Fury section | 21 |
| Cultures/religions, map, events, landed titles | 53 |
| Per-empire title renames (`e_scandinavia` 38, `e_wendish_empire` 32, `e_byzantium` 26, `e_arabia` 22, `e_germania` 22, …) | ~300 |

The README also **lacks the headline metric entirely**:

# Curated session knowledge base

This is the cleaned, navigable version of the session records. The nine original transcripts were shredded into these notes on 2026-09-21 and deleted from the repository root; the routing map and Git recovery point are in [`14-transcript-provenance.md`](14-transcript-provenance.md). No raw transcripts are stored here — the archive navigates conclusions, procedures and evidence, not tool chatter.

## Latest verification and work

[**Family references and exceptional claim provenance**](13-family-and-exceptional-claim-provenance.md)
— third bounded 3g pass: 18 selected cases with current family context and five
selected title-definition corpora. Ten parent-verified transitions and eight
earliest-state observations; six deterministic outputs and 139 passing tests.
[Evidence](family-claim-crosscheck/README.md) · [Validation](audits/family-claim-validation.json) ·
[Current queue](04-open-work.md).

Earlier stages: [employer/date register](12-employer-date-provenance-and-register.md),
[first upstream cross-check](11-upstream-crosscheck.md), [broader effects](10-broader-effects-review.md),

[interruption audit](06-interruption-audit.md). The nine original transcripts
were shredded into these notes (see
[14-transcript-provenance.md](14-transcript-provenance.md)). Historical/runtime
questions remain open.

## Preservation policy

- Original game/mod/source files are untouched.
- The nine session transcripts were shredded into curated notes on 2026-09-21 and deleted from the repository root; the routing map and the Git recovery point are in [14-transcript-provenance.md](14-transcript-provenance.md).
- Curated notes preserve important conclusions, procedures, caveats, and provenance without preserving repetitive tool narration.
- The SHA manifests, reports, and hashing tool remain in their own subfolders.

## Canonical source paths

The three character-history trees, all **read-only**:

| Role | Path | Files | Top-level IDs |
|---|---|---:|---:|
| vanilla baseline (pristine) | `Crusader Kings II/history/characters/` | 117 | 37,699 |
| repaired working copy (17 ID fixes, `reports/ID_FIXES.md`) | `characters/` | 117 | 37,700 |
| CleanSlate (unpacked from `CleanSlate-main.zip`) | `CleanSlate/CleanSlate/history/characters/` | 117 | 37,699 |

The `CleanSlate/` tree is an in-repo, byte-exact unpack of `CleanSlate-main.zip`
(SHA-256 `95c7892de33b82c13a2c5b9e3e20ed3fdeb94785a4f201a2ae26daa33582feee`, itself
byte-identical to upstream `7ee3cbae` per the root README). Upstream repo layout is
kept: the mod proper is the nested `CleanSlate/` folder next to `CleanSlate.mod`.
From this records directory, `python3 tools/charhist.py extract` verifies an existing
tree without writing; it refuses mismatches. Only a missing tree is extracted.

`master_changelog.txt` (the live CleanSlate changelog) lives at
`CleanSlate/CleanSlate/master_changelog.txt`; the frozen GitHub front page at
`CleanSlate/README.md`.

## Transcript provenance

The nine root-level session transcripts were shredded into this knowledge
base on 2026-09-21 and deleted from the working tree. Every session's content
is routed through the map in
[`14-transcript-provenance.md`](14-transcript-provenance.md); the originals
remain recoverable from Git history (`git show 78de990:log4.txt`, etc.).
Mentions of `log2.txt` / `log3,*` / `log4.txt` inside the earlier reports
refer to those now-deleted files. The curated notes are the working interface.

## Status vocabulary

- **Verified** — directly checked against repository/upstream data and reproducible.
- **Observed** — directly seen, but not yet generalized or independently rerun.
- **Reported** — present in an earlier session report; needs recheck before being treated as fact.
- **Open** — specifically deferred or incomplete.

# Upstream and changelog cross-check — step 3g, first bounded pass

**2026-09-20 · CK2 3.3.5.1 · source provenance, not an engine test**

[Previous: broader effects](10-broader-effects-review.md) ·
[Evidence guide](upstream-crosscheck/README.md) ·
[Validation](audits/crosscheck-validation.json) · [Remaining work](04-open-work.md)

## What this pass establishes

The priority effects now have commit-level provenance, and some earlier family
and employer findings have a stronger history trail. **The entire comparison is
not finished:** this is the first bounded 3g pass, not a claim that every residual
character, historical question or runtime behavior is resolved.

- **Batu's ownership-scope/template rewrite and all three army retargets date to
  June 2021**, in a commit titled “More character history formatting.” The later
  2024 message about troop corrections does not explain these particular edits.
- The six opinion rewrites and two Sindhi dead-inclusive effects **do** date to
  the June 2024 consistency pass. The commit identifies the implementation date,
  but does not give a separate explanation for every edited line.
- **William's fleet has an additional destination change:** a numeric province
  becomes `closest_to_capital`. The army-only location table in report 10 was not
  a complete fleet-location assessment.
- **Global flags need a multi-file, multi-date account.** Temujin's birth flag
  moves to title history in 2022; his unification flag already existed there.
  William's flag first moves to title history, then is removed as unused in 2024.
- Missing employer **248075 → 1060080** is already present at the March 2021
  import and persists through every captured revision. Johannes's corrected
  employer **30444** is older still: present in the earliest German path revision
  checked, **April 2020**.

Coverage: **21 selected characters**, ten character paths plus two title paths,
**104 snapshots**, 27 distinct commits, and **30 direct-parent file comparisons**.
The separate additional-effects scan still covers **all 117 files in all three
local trees**. All source trees remain untouched.

## 1. How the provenance was checked

The fixed upstream reference is
[`7ee3cbaeeb0d913dd4bfecd65cae84cf007d3e9c`](https://github.com/ck2plus/CleanSlate/commit/7ee3cbaeeb0d913dd4bfecd65cae84cf007d3e9c),
matching the supplied CleanSlate snapshot. No assumption is made that a mutable
GitHub `main` still points there.

For each selected path, the collector paginates GitHub's path history at that
reference, reads actual Git blobs and keeps full selected character excerpts
(or the complete two title files). It verifies blob object hashes and compares
all twelve pinned files with local files after decoding and CRLF→LF conversion.
This is **decoded-content equality**, not a false assertion that UTF-8 Git blobs
and CP1252 working-tree bytes must be identical.

For every selected syntax-tree transition, the immediate commit parent is also
fetched. Its full-file Git blob matches the preceding path snapshot. Thus the
identified edits below are not merely changes somewhere between two arbitrarily
chosen revisions. Merge-parent ambiguity would stop this collector for review.

The scope is the returned histories of **fixed paths**, not rename-following or
an exhaustive audit of every repository branch. The earliest revision differs
by file: German history predates the bulk March 2021 import. Full source excerpts,
line spans, hashes, commit parents and messages are preserved in the

=== upstream-crosscheck/README.md (head 50) ===
# Step 3g evidence — first bounded upstream cross-check

[Report 11](../11-upstream-crosscheck.md) ·
[Validation](../audits/crosscheck-validation.json) · [Queue](../04-open-work.md)

Two separate processes are kept distinct:

1. **Network capture:** read selected fixed-path histories at pinned upstream
   `7ee3cbaeeb0d913dd4bfecd65cae84cf007d3e9c`, verify Git blobs, retain excerpts,
   parents, dates and messages. No checkout, branch switch or source edits.
2. **Offline analysis:** validate the frozen capture and prior stage inputs,
   produce deterministic provenance tables, scan additional commands across all
   three 117-file character trees and route the 147 prior effect-entry rows.

Neither is a CK2 interpreter or independent historical research.

## Files and scope

| File | Rows / coverage | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| [upstream-evidence.json](upstream-evidence.json) | 12 paths, 27 commits, 104 snapshots, 30 parent checks, 66 unique excerpts | Frozen network evidence, selected full character blocks and two full title files; blob and excerpt hashes |
| [revision-blocks.csv](revision-blocks.csv) | 217 | Per-character/file snapshot states, text/tree fingerprints, full-file line spans via capture and GitHub links |
| [snapshot-fields.csv](snapshot-fields.csv) | 2,072 | Selected fields, scope paths and full-file line numbers across revisions; duplicate fields retained |
| [upstream-tree-changes.csv](upstream-tree-changes.csv) | 41 | Selected syntax-tree transitions with verified immediate parent and previous path commit |
| [upstream-tree-diffs.md](upstream-tree-diffs.md) | Same 41 transitions | Readable excerpt diffs; hunk offsets are excerpt-relative, not original full-file lines |
| [additional-effect-changes.csv](additional-effect-changes.csv) | 15 | All-file, all-three-tree extra-command comparison; all current deltas are CS vs vanilla |
| [effect-payload-triage.csv](effect-payload-triage.csv) | 147 | Routes every prior changed effect entry to evidence categories; unknown keys stay visible |
| [changelog-crossrefs.csv](changelog-crossrefs.csv) | 16 | Exact focused search terms, matching line/text or an explicit no-literal-match result |
| [run.json](run.json) | — | Frozen capture, changelog, prior manifests, tools and seven output hashes |

The selected character paths are **basque, bodpa, german, kurdish, mongol, norman,
norse, occitan, panjabi, sindhi** (21 characters total). The title paths are
**e_mongol_empire** and **k_england**. Exact ID selections are in the capture's
`scope` object. This is not provenance coverage for every changed character or
all 117 files. The additional-command scan is all-file; the upstream study is not.

The 104 snapshots comprise 92 path revisions and twelve pinned endpoints. Parent
checks are a separate set of thirty file comparisons, not thirty additional
independent commits. The earliest path revision is not globally fixed: German
history begins earlier than the March 2021 bulk import in this capture.

## Important interpretation rules

- GitHub commit lists are fully paginated for the selected fixed paths/ref. The
  collector does not follow file renames or analyze every branch. An unsupported
  merge-parent case stops selected-transition publication for review.
- Actual Git blob bytes are fetched and their object SHA verified. The Contents
  API is used to locate the blob, not assumed to provide byte-exact blob content.
  Git-blob bytes may be CP1252 or UTF-8; local pinned comparison is decoded text
  with line endings normalized, not raw-byte equality across encoding contracts.

# Method — how these numbers were produced

← [Findings](README.md) · [Archive index](../README.md)

Every figure in this archive comes from scripted analysis of three sources, so it can be
re-run and challenged.

## Sources

| Source | Role |
|---|---|
| `CleanSlate-main.zip` → unpacked | the artifact under analysis, 6,419 files |
| `git clone https://github.com/ck2plus/CleanSlate` | provenance: 2,172 commits, tags, authorship |
| `Crusader Kings II/` (this repo) | vanilla **3.3.5.1** diff baseline |

**Provenance check first.** Before analysing anything I confirmed the uploaded zip matches
upstream `HEAD`:

```bash
diff -rq CleanSlate-main upstream -x .git    # -> 0 differences
git -C upstream log -1 --format='%H %ad %s'  # 7ee3cbae 2026-06-29 "Fixed iqta tax/levy laws not revoking _8"
```

This matters: it means findings apply to the live project, not a stale snapshot.

## Parsing CK2 script

CK2's format is brace-nested `key = value`. Naive regex over it produces wrong answers —
my first duplicate-event pass reported **3,567 collisions** because `id = X` appears both
in event *definitions* and in nested `character_event = { id = X }` *calls*.

So I wrote a small depth-aware tokenizer (`ck2parse.py`) that:

- strips `#` comments while respecting quoted strings
- tokenizes into `{`, `}`, bare words and quoted strings
- yields **depth-0** `keyword = { … }` blocks only
- reads fields at depth 0 of a block body (`direct_field`)

Re-run with correct parsing, duplicates went **3,567 → 0**. Every structural claim uses
this parser, not line-based grep.

Encoding: all script read as **cp1252** with replacement, matching what the game does.

## What each check does

| Check | Approach |
|---|---|
| **Brace balance** | comment-stripped `count('{') - count('}')` per file |
| **Duplicate event IDs** | depth-0 blocks of the 6 event types → `id` field → count collisions |
| **Dangling event refs** | regex for `*_event = { … id = N }` across *all* `.txt`, minus defined set |
| **Loc integrity** | parse `;`-delimited CSVs → key set; extract `desc`/`name`/`title`/`tooltip`/… refs from `events/` + `decisions/`; subtract keys, custom-loc commands, scope words |
| **Title integrity** | `^\s*([ekdcb]_\w+)\s*=\s*\{` in `common/landed_titles/` vs `history/titles/*.txt` filenames |
| **Province integrity** | `map/definition.csv` IDs vs `history/provinces/` filename prefixes |
| **Rename validation** | parse `- old -> new` from changelog; assert `new` exists and `old` is gone |
| **Style adoption** | comment-stripped occurrence counts, mod vs vanilla, same folder scope |
| **Encoding** | flag files whose non-ASCII bytes decode as valid UTF-8 (should be cp1252); classify comment-only vs live code |

## Corrections made during analysis

Recorded because they affect how much to trust each number.

1. **Duplicate events 3,567 → 0.** Naive regex counted nested event *calls* as
   definitions. Fixed with the depth-aware parser.
2. **Dangling refs 1 → 0.** Regex `([A-Za-z_]*\.?\d+)` truncated `CK2.18000` to `CK2`.
   Tightened to `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z_0-9]*\.\d+|\d+`.
3. **"Dead" scripted effects 141 → 87 → contextualised.** First pass only matched
   `name = yes|{` call syntax. Re-done as whole-corpus word frequency (occurrence count
   ≤ 1 ⇒ only its own definition). **Still not reported as a defect**: CleanSlate is
   explicitly a *modding resource*, so unused-but-exported helpers
   (`add_trait_silently_*`, `*_score`) are plausibly deliberate API surface. Reporting
   them as dead code would be a false positive — flagged as a question for the authors
   instead, not a finding.
4. **`use_first_valid` "0" → 777.** My folder scope excluded `localisation/`, where the
   idiom actually lives. Corrected before publishing.
5. **`age = 16` 82 remaining → not violations.** Manual inspection showed all are
   `min_age = 16` event fields or `common/bookmarks/` character ages, where a literal is
   the only valid syntax.

## Deliberate limitations

- **Baseline is 3.3.5.1, the same version the mod targets** (confirmed from the version
  string in `CK2game.exe`, not `ChangeLog.txt`, which stops at 3.3.3 because Paradox never
  documented the 3.3.4/3.3.5 hotfixes). Diffs are therefore exact — no Paradox-side
  changes are misattributed to CleanSlate.
- **This repo's vanilla has 9,568 zero-byte art stubs.** Art/audio can't be diffed; 43 mod
  files fall in a `VANILLA_STUB` bucket and are excluded from content comparisons. This
  does **not** affect any script-level finding: every script format is intact (5,195 of
  5,225 `.txt`, and all `.csv`/`.gui`/`.lua`/`.info`), and `map/` — whose `.bmp` files
  carry logic, not art — is complete. Quantified in the
  [findings appendix](README.md#appendix-does-the-zero-byte-art-stubbing-limit-this-analysis).
- **No game execution.** Everything is static analysis — this sandbox can't run CK2, so
  runtime-only bugs (MTTH behaviour, AI weighting, actual tooltip rendering) are out of
  scope. Findings are limited to what's provable from the files.
- **Semantic correctness is not checked.** I can prove `EVTTOOLTIPHF20329` is undefined; I
  cannot prove a trigger encodes the *intended* game logic.

Scratch workspace (intentionally outside git, to keep generated data out of the repo):

```
├── eventdelta.json         events added/removed vs vanilla
├── loc_missing.json        unresolved localisation references
└── deadcode.json           single-occurrence scripted effects/triggers
```

"""Small, strict Clausewitz token/tree reader for offline comparison.

Keeps order, duplicate fields, quoted string contents, offsets and comments.
Does not interpret game effects or repair malformed input. Input is decoded by
its caller; quoted escapes are preserved rather than guessed/unescaped.
"""
from dataclasses import dataclass
import re


class ParseError(ValueError):
    pass


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class Token:
    text: str
    line: int
    start: int
    end: int
    kind: str = 'atom'

    @property
    def value(self):
        return self.text[1:-1] if self.kind == 'string' else self.text


@dataclass(slots=True)
class Node:
    key: Token
    op: str
    value: object  # Token or list[Node]; bare list atoms have op == ''
    end: Token

    @property
    def group(self):
        return isinstance(self.value, list)


PATTERN = re.compile(r'(?P<space>\s+)|(?P<comment>\#[^\n]*)|'
                     r'(?P<string>"(?:\\.|[^"\\])*")|'
                     r'(?P<symbol>[{}=<>!]+)|(?P<atom>[^\s{}=<>!"#]+)')


def lex(text, filename='<text>'):
    tokens, comments = [], []
    pos, line = 0, 1
    while pos < len(text):
        m = PATTERN.match(text, pos)
        if not m:
            raise ParseError(f'{filename}:{line}: unexpected/unclosed token at {text[pos:pos+30]!r}')
        kind, raw = m.lastgroup, m[0]
        if kind == 'symbol':
            # Braces are separate tokens even in "}}". Comparators stay intact.
            for part in re.finditer(r'[{}]|>=|<=|!=|[=<>!]', raw):
                tokens.append(Token(part[0], line, pos+part.start(), pos+part.end(), 'symbol'))
        elif kind != 'space':
            t = Token(raw, line, pos, m.end(), kind)
            (comments if kind == 'comment' else tokens).append(t)
        line += raw.count('\n')
        pos = m.end()
    return tokens, comments


def parse(text, filename='<text>'):
    tokens, comments = lex(text, filename)
    index = 0

    def error(message, t=None):
        raise ParseError(f'{filename}:{t.line if t else "EOF"}: {message}')

    def sequence(nested=False):
        nonlocal index
        out = []
        while index < len(tokens):
            key = tokens[index]
            if key.text == '}':
                if not nested:
                    error('unexpected closing brace', key)
                index += 1
                return out, key
            if key.kind == 'symbol':
                error('expected key or list atom', key)
            index += 1
            if index < len(tokens) and tokens[index].text in ('=', '>', '<', '>=', '<=', '!='):
                op = tokens[index].text
                index += 1
                if index >= len(tokens):
                    error('assignment missing value', key)
                value = tokens[index]
                index += 1
                if value.text == '{':
                    children, end = sequence(True)
                    out.append(Node(key, op, children, end))
                elif value.kind == 'symbol':
                    error('assignment missing scalar/group value', value)
                else:
                    out.append(Node(key, op, value, value))
            else:
                # Bare troop counts / flags are legal within list blocks.
                if not nested:
                    error('top-level item must be an assignment', key)
                out.append(Node(key, '', key, key))
        if nested:
            error('unclosed group')
        return out, None

    nodes, _ = sequence()
    return nodes, comments


def walk(nodes, path=()):
    """Every assignment, with ordered scope keys (no semantic scope inference)."""
    for node in nodes:
        yield path, node
        if node.group:
            yield from walk(node.value, path + (node.key.value,))
=== tools/README.md ===
# Tools — the comparison workbench

Python 3.10+, stdlib only. Run from the repository root. Everything here is
read-only towards the game, the mod and the repaired trees; generated output
goes to the records `comparison/` folder (or `--outdir` scratch space).

## The engine

| Tool | Role |
|---|---|
| `charhist.py` | the character-history comparison: `extract` / `inventory` / `comments` / `comment-diff` / `renames` / `classify` / `relationships` / `all` |
| `ck2script.py` | the strict Clausewitz token/tree parser everything shares (order, duplicate fields, comments, offsets preserved) |
| `rename_rules.py` | pinned changelog spellings and curated implementation renames, applied per-field only |
| `ck2-hash.py` · [ck2-hash.md](ck2-hash.md) | full-install SHA-256 manifests (copy of the root tool) |

## Stage review generators (offline, deterministic)

| Tool | Stage |
|---|---|
| `family_review.py` | 3d follow-up: scope tracing and chronology candidates |
| `employer_claim_date_review.py` | 3e: employers, claims, direct dates — all character files |
| `effects_review.py` | 3f: selected commands and complete effect-entry payloads |
| `crosscheck_review.py` | 3g pass 1: source/provenance triage from frozen upstream evidence |
| `case_register_review.py` | 3g pass 2: employer/date provenance and the working register |
| `family_claim_review.py` | 3g pass 3: family/exceptional-claim addendum |
| `provenance_selection.py` | the explicit, reproducible case selection for pass 2 |
| `audit_log3.py` | the read-only interruption audit of the recovered log3 analysis |

## Upstream capture (network, frozen evidence)

`crosscheck_upstream.py`, `register_upstream.py`, `family_claim_upstream.py`,
`family_upstream.py`, `claim_title_upstream.py` — read-only `gh` provenance
captures at the pinned upstream snapshot; their outputs are the frozen JSON in
the evidence folders, not live state.

## Validators and tests

`validate_comparison.py`, `validate_family.py`, `validate_family_claim.py`,
`validate_employer_claim_date.py`, `validate_effects_review.py`,
`validate_crosscheck.py`, `validate_case_register.py` — check published
artifacts against current inputs; never claim CK2 execution.

`test_*.py` — the regression suite:

```bash
python3 -m unittest discover -s CleanSlate-Archive/06-session-records/tools -p 'test_*.py'
```

Full reproduction workflow (two-seed byte-identity, validation) is documented
in [03-methods-and-reproduction.md](../03-methods-and-reproduction.md).

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Read-only pinned provenance for remaining family references and exceptional claims.
Uses existing verified-blob/rename readers without changing earlier stage inputs.
"""
import argparse
from collections import defaultdict
from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor
from datetime import datetime, timezone
import json
from pathlib import Path

import charhist as ch
import employer_claim_date_review as ed
import crosscheck_upstream as up
import register_upstream as lineage


def cases():
    result=[{'case_id':'father:'+bid,'id':bid,'file':fn,'selector':'father','targets':[],
             'category':category} for bid,fn,category in (
        ('248748','kanuri.txt','undefined father'),('273088','maghreb_arabic.txt','undefined father'),
        ('248207','mongol.txt','Mani chronology mismatch'),('248208','mongol.txt','Mani chronology mismatch'),
        ('248206','mongol.txt','nearby corrected-father control'))]
    for bid in ('3853','32965','32978','32993','32994','32995','32996','32997'):
        result.append({'case_id':'claim:tangier:'+bid,'id':bid,'file':'maghreb_arabic.txt','selector':'claims',
            'targets':['d_tangiers','d_tangier','c_tangier'],'category':'Tangier claim spelling/rank evidence'})
    for bid,fn,targets,category in (
        ('141','norman.txt',['k_england'],'removed England claim'),
        ('144','norman.txt',['k_england'],'removed England claim'),
        ('248149','han.txt',['c_maqen'],'disabled missing-title claim'),
        ('34014','bedouin_arabic.txt',['d_hamadan','d_jibal'],'obsolete-title claim retarget'),
        ('248197','mongol.txt',['d_kabul','d_kabulistan'],'endpoint-equal claim with internal upstream retarget')):
        result.append({'case_id':'claim:'+bid,'id':bid,'file':fn,'selector':'claims','targets':targets,'category':category})
    return sorted(result,key=lambda r:(int(r['id']),r['case_id']))


def collect(cache):
    selected=cases();scope=defaultdict(set)
    for c in selected:scope[c['file']].add(c['id'])
    scope['mongol.txt'].update(('248204','248205','248210','248211'))
    scope['manichean_church.txt'].add('248201')
    scope={fn:sorted(ids,key=int) for fn,ids in sorted(scope.items())}
    histories={};commits={};renames=[];requests=[]
    for filename,ids in scope.items():
        path='CleanSlate/history/characters/'+filename
        seq=lineage.lineage(path,up.SNAPSHOT,cache,commits,renames);histories[filename]=seq
        for revision in seq+[{'path':path,'commit':up.SNAPSHOT}]:requests.append((filename,revision,ids))
    def fetch(args):
        fn,rev,ids=args;s=up.snapshot(rev['path'],rev['commit'],ids,cache);s['logical_file']=fn;return s
    with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=4) as pool:snapshots=list(pool.map(fetch,requests))
    by_ref={(s['logical_file'],s['path'],s['commit']):s for s in snapshots}
    parent_requests=[]
    for filename,seq in histories.items():
        for old,new in zip(seq,seq[1:]):
            a=by_ref[filename,old['path'],old['commit']];b=by_ref[filename,new['path'],new['commit']]
            if any(lineage.signature(a['blocks'][bid])!=lineage.signature(b['blocks'][bid]) for bid in scope[filename]):
                parents=commits[new['commit']]['parents']
                if len(parents)!=1:raise ValueError('merge-parent review required')
                parent_requests.append((filename,{'path':old['path'],'commit':parents[0]},scope[filename],old,new))
    with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=4) as pool:parents=list(pool.map(lambda a:fetch(a[:3]),parent_requests))
    for p,args in zip(parents,parent_requests):
        old,new=args[3:];p['for_commit']=new['commit'];p['previous_path_commit']=old['commit']
        p['matches_previous_path_blob']=p['git_blob']==by_ref[args[0],old['path'],old['commit']]['git_blob']
        if not p['matches_previous_path_blob']:raise ValueError('actual parent differs from preceding path revision')
    excerpts={}
    for s in snapshots+parents:
        for b in s['blocks'].values():
            if b is not None:
                text=b.pop('text');sha=up.digest(text.encode());excerpts[sha]=text;b['excerpt_sha256']=sha
    return {'version':1,'review_date':'2026-09-21','retrieved_at_utc':datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(),
        'upstream':up.UPSTREAM,'pinned_snapshot':up.SNAPSHOT,'selection':selected,'scope':scope,
        'histories_oldest_first':histories,'commits':commits,'verified_renames':renames,
        'snapshots':snapshots,'parent_checks':parents,'excerpts':excerpts,
        'tools_sha256':{n:ch.sha(Path(__file__).with_name(n)) for n in ('family_claim_upstream.py','crosscheck_upstream.py','register_upstream.py')},
        'limits':'Selected character lineages and verified parents. Current reference absence is not a scan of every historic definition tree. No engine run or invented genealogy.'}


def main():
    ap=argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    ap.add_argument('--cache',type=Path,default=Path('/home/user/ck2-family-claims/api-cache'))

=== audits/README.md ===
# Audits — machine validation records

Frozen JSON records produced by the validators in [`../tools/`](../tools/).
Each one pins what a stage's outputs claimed at publication time; rerunning
the validators regenerates the checks (commands in
[03-methods-and-reproduction.md](../03-methods-and-reproduction.md)).

| Record | What it pins |
|---|---|
| `repair-validation.json` | the v2 comparison regeneration: comment rows, file classification, input files, missing family targets |
| `family-validation.json` | the family-scan outputs and candidate counts (counts are candidates, not bugs) |
| `employer-claim-date-validation.json` | step-3e outputs: employer/claim/life-event change counts and definition scans |
| `effects-validation.json` | step-3f outputs: spawn slots, killer deltas, Batu model notes |
| `crosscheck-validation.json` | first 3g pass: offline output hashes and the extra effect-delta rows |
| `case-register-validation.json` | second 3g pass: register coverage of employer rows and carried-forward cohorts |
| `family-claim-validation.json` | third 3g pass: the 18-case addendum, lineages and earliest-state cases |
| `log3-checks.json` | the original interruption-audit checks (historical) |
| `log3-post-repair-checks.json` | those same checks rerun after the comparison repair |

These are evidence, not live state: read them, don't edit them.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Read-only gh provenance capture for selected histories at the pinned snapshot.
Keeps all path revisions, commit parents and selected full block excerpts; no checkout.
Network capture is intentionally separate from deterministic offline analysis.
"""
import argparse
import base64
from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor
from datetime import datetime, timezone
import hashlib
import json
from pathlib import Path
import subprocess
from urllib.parse import quote

import charhist as ch
import employer_claim_date_review as ed
from family_upstream import UPSTREAM, SNAPSHOT

CASES = {
    'mongol.txt': ['125501','125503'],
    'norse.txt': ['102507','144001','163111','163112'],
    'norman.txt': ['140'],
    'bodpa.txt': ['247105','247227','252020'],
    'basque.txt': ['168104'],
    'occitan.txt': ['190480','190484'],
    'sindhi.txt': ['178210','178224'],
    'panjabi.txt': ['248075'],
    'german.txt': ['7658','7657','163047','170341'],
    'kurdish.txt': ['2601031'],
}


def digest(raw): return hashlib.sha256(raw).hexdigest()


def decode(raw):
    try: return raw.decode('utf-8').replace('\r\n','\n'), 'utf-8'
    except UnicodeDecodeError: return raw.decode('cp1252').replace('\r\n','\n'), 'cp1252'


def api(endpoint, cache):
    name=digest(endpoint.encode())+'.json';p=cache/name
    if p.exists(): return json.loads(p.read_text())
    raw=subprocess.check_output(['gh','api','repos/'+UPSTREAM+'/'+endpoint],timeout=90)
    result=json.loads(raw)
    ch.write_text(cache,name,json.dumps(result,ensure_ascii=False,sort_keys=True)+'\n')
    return result


def path_history(path, cache):
    result=[];page=1
    while True:
        batch=api('commits?path='+quote(path)+'&sha='+SNAPSHOT+'&per_page=100&page='+str(page),cache)
        if not isinstance(batch,list): raise ValueError('unexpected commit response')
        result.extend(batch)
        if len(batch)<100: break
        page+=1
    if len({c['sha'] for c in result})!=len(result): raise ValueError('duplicate history commit')
    return result


def snapshot(path, commit, ids, cache):
    meta=api('contents/'+quote(path)+'?ref='+commit,cache)
    # Contents API may transcode legacy encodings. Hash/parse the actual Git blob.
    blob=api('git/blobs/'+meta['sha'],cache)
    if blob.get('encoding')!='base64': raise ValueError('unsupported blob encoding')
    raw=base64.b64decode(blob['content'],validate=False)
    git_blob=hashlib.sha1(b'blob '+str(len(raw)).encode()+b'\0'+raw).hexdigest()
    if git_blob!=meta['sha']: raise ValueError('blob integrity mismatch')
    text,encoding=decode(raw);doc=ch.document(text) if ids else None;lines=text.splitlines()
    blocks={}
    if ids is None:
        blocks['@file']={'line':1,'end_line':len(lines),'text':text}
    for bid in ids or []:
        n=doc.blocks.get(bid)
        blocks[bid]=None if n is None else {'line':n.key.line,'end_line':n.end.line,
            'text':'\n'.join(lines[n.key.line-1:n.end.line])+'\n'}
    result={'commit':commit,'path':path,'git_blob':git_blob,'raw_sha256':digest(raw),
        'encoding':encoding,'decoded_lf_sha256':digest(text.encode()),'blocks':blocks}
    if commit==SNAPSHOT:
        local=ch.REPO/'CleanSlate'/path
        local_text,_=decode(local.read_bytes())
        if local_text!=text: raise ValueError('pinned upstream/local decoded file mismatch: '+path)
        result['local_sha256']=ch.sha(local)
    return result


def collect(cache):
    histories={};requests=[];commits={}
    paths={'CleanSlate/history/characters/'+fn:ids for fn,ids in CASES.items()}
    paths.update({'CleanSlate/history/titles/'+fn:None for fn in ('e_mongol_empire.txt','k_england.txt')})
    for path,ids in sorted(paths.items()):
        history=path_history(path,cache)
        if not history: raise ValueError('empty history: '+path)
        histories[path]=[c['sha'] for c in history]
        for c in history:
            commits[c['sha']]={'sha':c['sha'],'date':c['commit']['author']['date'],
                'message':c['commit']['message'],'parents':[p['sha'] for p in c['parents']],
                'url':c['html_url']}
        for commit in [*reversed(histories[path]),SNAPSHOT]: requests.append((path,commit,ids))
    with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=4) as pool:
        snapshots=list(pool.map(lambda args:snapshot(*args,cache),requests))
    # Verify direct parents for selected AST transitions, not just adjacent path commits.
    by_ref={(s['path'],s['commit']):s for s in snapshots}
    parent_requests=[]
    def signature(block):
        return None if block is None else tuple(ed.raw_node(n) for n in ch.parse(block['text'])[0])
    for path,history in histories.items():
        chronological=list(reversed(history))
        for old,new in zip(chronological,chronological[1:]):
            a,b=by_ref[path,old],by_ref[path,new]
            if any(signature(a['blocks'][bid])!=signature(b['blocks'][bid]) for bid in a['blocks']):
                parents=commits[new]['parents']
                if len(parents)!=1: raise ValueError('selected transition needs explicit merge-parent review: '+new)
                parent_requests.append((path,parents[0],paths[path],new,old))
    with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=4) as pool:
        parents=list(pool.map(lambda args:snapshot(*args[:3],cache),parent_requests))
    for parent,request in zip(parents,parent_requests):
        parent['for_commit']=request[3];parent['previous_path_commit']=request[4]
        parent['matches_previous_path_blob']=parent['git_blob']==by_ref[parent['path'],request[4]]['git_blob']
        if not parent['matches_previous_path_blob']:
            raise ValueError('path sequence differs from direct parent; explicit review required')
    # Deduplicate full excerpts, preserving source spans in each revision.
    excerpts={}
    for snap in snapshots+parents:
        for block in snap['blocks'].values():
            if block is not None:
                text=block.pop('text');sha=digest(text.encode());excerpts[sha]=text;block['excerpt_sha256']=sha
    return {'version':1,'review_date':'2026-09-20','retrieved_at_utc':datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(),
        'upstream':UPSTREAM,'pinned_snapshot':SNAPSHOT,'tool_sha256':ch.sha(Path(__file__)),
        'scope':CASES,'title_paths':['CleanSlate/history/titles/e_mongol_empire.txt','CleanSlate/history/titles/k_england.txt'],'path_histories_newest_first':histories,'commits':commits,
        'snapshots':snapshots,'parent_checks':parents,'excerpts':excerpts,
        'limits':'All returned commits for ten character paths and two title paths at the pinned ref, not rename-following or all repository history. Adjacent path snapshots bound changes; inspect commit parents before claiming exact introduction. Generic messages do not establish per-line intent. No runtime tests.'}


def main():
    ap=argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    ap.add_argument('--cache',type=Path,default=Path('/home/user/ck2-crosscheck/api-cache'))
    ap.add_argument('--outdir',type=Path,default=ch.OUTDIR_DEFAULT/'upstream-crosscheck')
    args=ap.parse_args();out=ed.safe_destination(args.outdir);cache=ed.safe_destination(args.cache)
    result=collect(cache)
    ch.write_text(out,'upstream-evidence.json',json.dumps(result,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2,sort_keys=True)+'\n')
    print(json.dumps({'paths':len(result['path_histories_newest_first']),'commits':len(result['commits']),
        'snapshots':len(result['snapshots']),'unique_excerpts':len(result['excerpts'])},indent=2))


if __name__=='__main__':main()

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Deterministic, conservative CK2 character-history comparison (stdlib only).

Analysis never writes source trees. `extract` verifies an existing extraction
without modifying it; an absent tree is staged/CRC-checked before publication.
`all` generates steps 1–3 plus the first relationship pass in temporary storage
and publishes only after all stages succeed. This is not a CK2 interpreter.
"""
import argparse
from collections import Counter
from dataclasses import dataclass
from difflib import SequenceMatcher
from functools import lru_cache
import csv
import hashlib
import io
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path, PurePosixPath
import re
import tempfile
import zipfile

from ck2script import ParseError, parse, walk
from rename_rules import DOC_RENAMES, IMPL_RENAMES

REPO = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3]
OUTDIR_DEFAULT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]

           'repaired': REPO / 'characters',
           'cleanslate': REPO / 'CleanSlate/CleanSlate/history/characters'}

    'frankish': 'french', 'old_frankish': 'frankish', 'saxon': 'anglo-saxon',
    'old_saxon': 'saxon', 'castillan': 'castilian', 'ugricbaltic': 'estonian',

               'trait': 'trait', 'add_trait': 'trait', 'remove_trait': 'trait'}
DATE = re.compile(r'^\d+\.\d+\.\d+$')
RELATION_KEYS = {'father', 'mother', 'add_spouse', 'remove_spouse',
                 'add_matrilineal_spouse', 'add_consort', 'remove_consort'}
VERSION = '2 (strict token/tree parser; conservative comparison)'


def read_text(path):
    # Latin-1 preserves each input byte. Only output is converted to CP1252 display.
    return Path(path).read_bytes().decode('latin-1').replace('\r\n', '\n')


def display(text):
    # Undefined CP1252 bytes are escaped, never silently replaced/lost.
    return text.encode('latin-1').decode('cp1252', errors='backslashreplace')


def sha(path):
    return hashlib.sha256(Path(path).read_bytes()).hexdigest()

| `CleanSlate/master_changelog.txt` (105 KB, buried in the mod folder) | **2026-06-29** | current, header still says "beta 6.1" |

Since the README froze, **983 commits** have changed game script. It is missing **922
lines** of changelog content, including:

- **116 further bugfixes**
- **323 renamed title IDs** — save-breaking and mod-breaking
- 86 deleted obsolete titles, 72 scripted-trigger/effect changes, 39 trait changes

A modder reading the GitHub page to check compatibility gets a document that predates most
of the breaking changes. This is a documentation failure sitting on top of genuinely
excellent engineering, and it's cheap to fix.

## Concrete findings to send upstream

Eight verified items, each reproducible. Ranked by value:

| # | Finding | Severity |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | README is 983 script-commits stale; two changelogs diverged | **high** — public-facing |
| 2 | `master_changelog.txt` header still says "beta 6.1"; 514 commits and a release since | medium |
| 3 | Changelog typo: `d_wallachia -> c_muntenia` should be **`d_muntenia`** | low, precise |
| 4 | Changelog typo: `d_pommerania -> c_pomerania` should be **`d_pomerania`** | low, precise |
| 5 | `EVTTOOLTIPHF20329` referenced in `HF_coronation_events.txt:8327`, defined nowhere | low, real |
| 5b | Two orphaned flags (`d_wallachia.tga`, `c_avanti.tga`) left behind by title renames | low, tidy-up |
| 6 | 21 files are UTF-8 in a Windows-1252 codebase; 1 has non-ASCII in live text | low |
| 7 | House style exists only in commit messages — no `CONTRIBUTING.md` | medium — costs review time |

Plus two observations that are opportunities rather than defects:

- **The event-naming migration is 2.5% done.** The authors defined a new convention
  (`evt_namespace_001_desc_a`) in `events/_documentation.info` and converted exactly four
  files — all `base_job_*`. 184 files remain fully legacy. It's a stalled pilot, and
  worth either finishing or explicitly parking.
- **`Schwarherz` and `schwarherz` are the same person** (same email) split across two
  rows in every contributor listing. A three-line `.mailmap` merges them — and would
  correctly rank them 2nd, not 3rd and 4th.

→ [All findings with evidence and fixes](../05-findings/README.md)

## What I'd tell the authors

The engineering is the hard part, and it's done well. The gap is that the project's
public face doesn't reflect the work: 983 commits of fixes are invisible to anyone who
hasn't cloned the repo and opened a 105 KB text file inside the mod directory.

The highest-leverage change is not code. It is making `master_changelog.txt` the README
(or generating one from the other), so the front page finally shows the five years of
work that actually happened.
=== changelog-audit.md typo section ===
**3 — Fix the header.** Set it to the version being worked toward, or `unreleased`, and
bump it as part of tagging.

**4 — Cut `1.0-beta7`.** 180 commits of finished work are release-invisible. Even a
pre-release tag would surface a year of fixes.

**5 — Surface the renames separately.** Given 327 title renames + 86 deletions, a
standalone `MIGRATION.md` (old ID → new ID, as a lookup table) would be the single most
useful artifact for downstream modders — more useful than prose changelog lines, because
it's greppable.

## Two concrete typos found while cross-checking

I validated all 327 documented title renames against the actual `landed_titles` and
`history/titles` contents. **325 are perfectly consistent.** Two have a wrong tier prefix:

| Changelog line | Problem | Correct |
|---|---|---|
| `- d_wallachia -> c_muntenia` | `c_muntenia` doesn't exist; it's a **duchy** | `d_wallachia -> d_muntenia` |
| `- d_pommerania -> c_pomerania` | `c_pomerania` doesn't exist; it's a **duchy** | `d_pommerania -> d_pomerania` |

Evidence:

```
common/landed_titles/e_carpathia.txt:626:   d_muntenia = {
localisation/00_landed_titles.csv:15325:    d_muntenia;Muntenia;...
history/titles/d_pomerania.txt              (exists)
grep -c 'c_muntenia|c_pomerania' across mod: 0
```

Both sit in a run of `c_`-prefixed neighbours in the changelog, so they look like
copy-paste slips in the document — the **code is correct**, only the changelog is wrong.
That a 327-entry hand-maintained rename list contains exactly two prefix typos is, frankly,
a good result.
